	csa_tree__ref #(
		.N(N + 1),
		.WIDTH_I(SUM_WIDTH + 1),
		.WIDTH_O(SUM_WIDTH + 1)
	) u_csa_tree(
		.operands_i(mantissa_comp),
		.sum_o(csa_sum),
		.carry_o(csa_carry)
	);
	wire [SUM_WIDTH:0] sum_result;
	assign sum_result = csa_sum + csa_carry;
	wire final_sign;
	wire [SUM_WIDTH - 1:0] sum_c;
	assign final_sign = sum_result[SUM_WIDTH];
	assign sum_c = (final_sign ? ~sum_result + 1 : sum_result[SUM_WIDTH - 1:0]);
	wire signed [EXP_WIDTH:0] rg_exp_adjust;
	wire signed [EXP_WIDTH:0] final_rg_exp;
	wire [SUM_WIDTH - 1:0] sum_norm;
	mantissa_norm__ref #(
		.WIDTH(SUM_WIDTH),
		.EXP_WIDTH(EXP_WIDTH),
		.DECIMAL_POINT(CARRY_WIDTH + 2)
	) u_mantissa_norm(
		.operand_i(sum_c),
		.exp_adjust(rg_exp_adjust),
		.result_o(sum_norm)
	);
	assign final_rg_exp = rg_exp_max + rg_exp_adjust;
	wire [MANT_WIDTH_O + 2:0] final_mant;
	generate
		if (SUM_WIDTH > (MANT_WIDTH_O + 3)) begin : genblk11
			wire [(SUM_WIDTH - MANT_WIDTH_O) - 3:0] sticky_bits;
			wire sticky_bit;
			assign sticky_bits = sum_norm[(SUM_WIDTH - MANT_WIDTH_O) - 3:0];
			assign sticky_bit = |sticky_bits;
			assign final_mant = {sum_norm[SUM_WIDTH - 1:(SUM_WIDTH - MANT_WIDTH_O) - 2], sticky_bit};
		end
		else begin : genblk11
			assign final_mant = sum_norm << ((MANT_WIDTH_O + 3) - SUM_WIDTH);
		end
	endgenerate
	posit_encoder__ref #(
		.n(n_o),
		.es(es_o),
		.EXP_WIDTH(EXP_WIDTH),
		.MANT_WIDTH(MANT_WIDTH_O + 2)
	) u_posit_encoder(
		.sign_i(final_sign),
		.rg_exp_i(final_rg_exp),
		.mant_norm_i(final_mant),
		.result_o(result_o)
	);
endmodule
module posit_decoder__ref (
	operand_i,
	sign_o,
	rg_exp_o,
	mant_norm_o
);
	parameter [31:0] n = 16;
	parameter [31:0] es = 1;
	function automatic integer pdpu_pkg_clog2;
		input integer n;
		begin
			n = n - 1;
			for (pdpu_pkg_clog2 = 0; n > 0; pdpu_pkg_clog2 = pdpu_pkg_clog2 + 1)
				n = n >> 1;
		end
	endfunction
	parameter [31:0] nd = pdpu_pkg_clog2(n - 1);
	parameter [31:0] EXP_WIDTH = nd + es;
	parameter [31:0] MANT_WIDTH = (n - es) - 3;
	input wire [n - 1:0] operand_i;
	output wire sign_o;
	output wire signed [EXP_WIDTH:0] rg_exp_o;
	output wire [MANT_WIDTH:0] mant_norm_o;
	wire sign;
	wire [n - 2:0] operand_value;
	assign sign = operand_i[n - 1];
	assign operand_value = (sign ? ~operand_i[n - 2:0] + 1 : operand_i[n - 2:0]);
	wire regS;
	wire [n - 2:0] lzc_operand;
	wire [nd - 1:0] leading_zero_count;
	wire lzc_zeroes;
	assign regS = operand_value[n - 2];
	assign lzc_operand = (regS ? ~operand_value : operand_value);
	lzc__ref #(
		.WIDTH(n - 1),
		.MODE(1'b1)
	) u_lzc(
		.in_i(lzc_operand),
		.cnt_o(leading_zero_count),
		.empty_o(lzc_zeroes)
	);
	wire [nd - 1:0] runlength;
	wire [nd - 1:0] regime_bits;
	wire signed [nd:0] regime_k;
	assign runlength = (lzc_zeroes ? n - 1 : leading_zero_count);
	assign regime_bits = (lzc_zeroes ? n - 1 : leading_zero_count + 1);
	assign regime_k = (regS ? {1'b0, runlength - 1} : {1'b1, ~runlength + 1});
	wire [n - 2:0] op_no_rg;
	barrel_shifter__ref #(
		.WIDTH(n - 1),
		.SHIFT_WIDTH(nd),
		.MODE(1'b0)
	) u_barrel_shifter(
		.operand_i(operand_value),
		.shift_amount(regime_bits),
		.result_o(op_no_rg)
	);
	assign sign_o = sign;
	wire [es:0] exp;
	generate
		if (es == 0) begin : genblk1
			assign exp = 0;
		end
		else begin : genblk1
			assign exp = op_no_rg[n - 2:((n - 2) - es) + 1];
		end
	endgenerate
	assign rg_exp_o = (regime_k << es) | exp;
	wire implicit_bit;
	assign implicit_bit = |operand_i[n - 2:0];
	assign mant_norm_o = {implicit_bit, op_no_rg[(n - 2) - es:2]};
endmodule
module posit_encoder__ref (
	sign_i,
	rg_exp_i,
	mant_norm_i,
	result_o
);
	parameter [31:0] n = 16;
	parameter [31:0] es = 1;
	function automatic integer pdpu_pkg_clog2;
		input integer n;
		begin
			n = n - 1;
			for (pdpu_pkg_clog2 = 0; n > 0; pdpu_pkg_clog2 = pdpu_pkg_clog2 + 1)
				n = n >> 1;
		end
	endfunction
	parameter [31:0] nd = pdpu_pkg_clog2(n - 1);
	parameter [31:0] EXP_WIDTH = nd + es;
	parameter [31:0] MANT_WIDTH = (n - es) - 3;
	input wire sign_i;
	input wire signed [EXP_WIDTH:0] rg_exp_i;
	input wire [MANT_WIDTH:0] mant_norm_i;
	output wire [n - 1:0] result_o;
	wire input_not_zero;
	assign input_not_zero = mant_norm_i[MANT_WIDTH];
	wire signed [EXP_WIDTH - es:0] regime_k;
	wire signed [es:0] exp;
	assign regime_k = rg_exp_i[EXP_WIDTH:es];
	generate
		if (es == 0) begin : genblk1
			assign exp = 0;
		end
		else begin : genblk1
			assign exp = rg_exp_i[es - 1:0];
		end
	endgenerate
	wire sign_k;
	wire [n - 2:0] rg_const;
	wire [n - 2:0] regime;
	assign sign_k = rg_exp_i[EXP_WIDTH];
	assign rg_const = 1;
	assign regime = (sign_k ? rg_const : ~rg_const);
	wire [EXP_WIDTH - es:0] regime_bits;
	assign regime_bits = (sign_k ? ~regime_k + 2 : regime_k + 2);
	localparam [31:0] TEMP_WIDTH = ((n - 1) + es) + MANT_WIDTH;
	wire [TEMP_WIDTH - 1:0] rg_exp_mant;
	generate
		if (es == 0) begin : genblk2
			assign rg_exp_mant = {regime, mant_norm_i[MANT_WIDTH - 1:0]};
		end
		else begin : genblk2
			assign rg_exp_mant = {regime, exp[es - 1:0], mant_norm_i[MANT_WIDTH - 1:0]};
		end
	endgenerate
	localparam [31:0] MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT = (MANT_WIDTH + es) + 1;
	localparam [31:0] SHIFT_WIDTH = pdpu_pkg_clog2(MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT + 1);
	wire [SHIFT_WIDTH - 1:0] shift_amount;
	assign shift_amount = (regime_bits >= n ? MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT : regime_bits + (((MANT_WIDTH + es) - n) + 1));
	wire [(TEMP_WIDTH + MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT) - 1:0] value_before_shift;
	wire [(TEMP_WIDTH + MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT) - 1:0] value_after_shift;
	assign value_before_shift = rg_exp_mant << MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT;
	barrel_shifter__ref #(
		.WIDTH(TEMP_WIDTH + MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT),
		.SHIFT_WIDTH(SHIFT_WIDTH),
		.MODE(1'b1)
	) u_barrel_shifter(
		.operand_i(value_before_shift),
		.shift_amount(shift_amount),
		.result_o(value_after_shift)
	);
	wire [n - 2:0] value_before_round;
	wire [MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT - 1:0] rounding_bits;
	assign {value_before_round, rounding_bits} = value_after_shift[(MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT + n) - 2:0];
	wire round_bit;
	wire sticky_bit;
	wire round_value;
	wire [n - 2:0] value_after_round;
	assign round_bit = rounding_bits[MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT - 1];
	assign sticky_bit = |rounding_bits[MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT - 2:0];
	assign round_value = round_bit & (sticky_bit | value_before_round[0]);
	assign value_after_round = value_before_round + round_value;
	wire [n - 1:0] normal_result;
	assign normal_result = (sign_i ? {1'b1, ~value_after_round + 1} : {1'b0, value_after_round});
	assign result_o = (input_not_zero ? normal_result : {n {1'sb0}});
endmodule
module radix4_booth_multiplier__ref (
	operand_a,
	operand_b,
	sum_o,
	carry_o
);
	parameter [31:0] WIDTH_A = 16;
	parameter [31:0] WIDTH_B = 16;
	parameter [31:0] WIDTH_O = WIDTH_A + WIDTH_B;
	input wire [WIDTH_A - 1:0] operand_a;
	input wire [WIDTH_B - 1:0] operand_b;
	output wire [WIDTH_O - 1:0] sum_o;
	output wire [WIDTH_O - 1:0] carry_o;
	localparam [31:0] COUNT = (WIDTH_B + 2) / 2;
	wire [(COUNT * WIDTH_O) - 1:0] partial_prods;
	gen_prods__ref #(
		.WIDTH_A(WIDTH_A),
		.WIDTH_B(WIDTH_B)
	) u_gen_prods(
		.operand_a(operand_a),
		.operand_b(operand_b),
		.partial_prods(partial_prods)
	);
	csa_tree__ref #(
		.N(COUNT),
		.WIDTH_I(WIDTH_O),
		.WIDTH_O(WIDTH_O)
	) u_csa_tree(
		.operands_i(partial_prods),
		.sum_o(sum_o),
		.carry_o(carry_o)
	);
endmodule

// PDPU dot-16, 5-stage pipelined -- readable SystemVerilog baseline.
//
// Source: github.com/qleenju/PDPU (ISCAS 2023, Apache-2.0), pdpu_top_pipelined,
// N default 4 -> 16, clock port clk -> clk to match the testbench.
//
// This is the ORIGINAL SystemVerilog, not sv2v output, so it keeps the upstream
// comments, the real generate-block names and `always_ff`. Only the changes
// yosys's front end actually requires were made:
//   * function bodies use `<name> = expr;` instead of `return expr;`
//   * `automatic` dropped from function-local variable declarations
//   * `unsigned'(x)` / `signed'(x)` -> `$unsigned(x)` / `$signed(x)`
//   * `'{a,b,c}` assignment patterns -> `{a,b,c}` (identical bit layout)
//   * simulation-only `// pragma translate_off` regions removed
//   * packed 2-D arrays lowered to flat vectors -- element e of `x` is
//     `x[e*W +: W]`, bit b of element e is `x[e*W + b]`. This is the ONE
//     construct yosys cannot handle (it cannot slice the outer dimension), and
//     the sole reason the sv2v detour existed.
//
// Module hierarchy, arithmetic and pipeline cut points are upstream verbatim.

// ---- FFNR: non-resettable flip-flop (from common_cells registers.svh) ----
`define FFNR(__q, __d, __clk)        \
  always_ff @(posedge (__clk)) begin \
    __q <= (__d);                    \
  end


package pdpu_pkg;
    // --------------
    // General Helper Function
    // ---------------
    function automatic int maximum(int a, int b);
        maximum = (a > b) ? a : b;
    endfunction

    function automatic integer clog2(input integer n);
        begin
            n = n-1;
            for(clog2=0; n>0; clog2=clog2+1)
                n = n>>1;
        end
    endfunction

endpackage

// Copyright 2016 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Copyright and related rights are licensed under the Solderpad Hardware

// Modified radix-4 booth wallace multiplier
module radix4_booth_multiplier #(
    parameter int unsigned WIDTH_A = 16,                // bit-width of operand A
    parameter int unsigned WIDTH_B = 16,                // bit-width of operand B
    parameter int unsigned WIDTH_O = WIDTH_A + WIDTH_B  // bit-width of the multiplication result
)(
    input logic [WIDTH_A-1:0] operand_a,
    input logic [WIDTH_B-1:0] operand_b,
    // the partial products are compressed into sum_o and carry_o
    output logic [WIDTH_O-1:0] sum_o,
    output logic [WIDTH_O-1:0] carry_o
);
    // count of partial products generated in unsigned multiplication
    localparam int unsigned COUNT = (WIDTH_B+2)/2;
    
    // ---------------
    // Generate partial products
    // ---------------
    logic [COUNT-1:0][WIDTH_O-1:0] partial_prods;
    gen_prods #(
        .WIDTH_A(WIDTH_A),
        .WIDTH_B(WIDTH_B)
    ) u_gen_prods(
        .operand_a(operand_a),
        .operand_b(operand_b),
        .partial_prods(partial_prods)
    );

    // ---------------
    // Compress the partial products into sum and carry
    // ---------------
    csa_tree #(
        .N(COUNT),
        .WIDTH_I(WIDTH_O),
        .WIDTH_O(WIDTH_O)
    ) u_csa_tree(
        .operands_i(partial_prods),
        .sum_o(sum_o),
        .carry_o(carry_o)
    );
endmodule

// barrel shifter
module barrel_shifter #(
    parameter int unsigned WIDTH = 8,               // bit-width of input data
    parameter int unsigned SHIFT_WIDTH = 3,         // bit-width of shift amount
    // MODE=0-->shift left; MODE=1-->shift right
    parameter bit MODE = 1'b0
)(
    input logic [WIDTH-1:0] operand_i,
    input logic [SHIFT_WIDTH-1:0] shift_amount,
    output logic [WIDTH-1:0] result_o
);
    logic [SHIFT_WIDTH-1:0][WIDTH-1:0] temp_results;

    assign temp_results[SHIFT_WIDTH-1] = operand_i;

    generate
        genvar i;
        // Left Shift
        if(MODE==1'b0) begin
            for(i=SHIFT_WIDTH-1;i>0;i--) begin
                assign temp_results[i-1] = shift_amount[i] ? temp_results[i]<<(2**i) : temp_results[i];
            end
            assign result_o = shift_amount[0] ? temp_results[0]<<1 : temp_results;
        end
        // Left Right
        else begin
            for(i=SHIFT_WIDTH-1;i>0;i--) begin
                assign temp_results[i-1] = shift_amount[i] ? temp_results[i]>>(2**i) : temp_results[i];
            end
            assign result_o = shift_amount[0] ? temp_results[0]>>1 : temp_results;
        end
    endgenerate
endmodule

/// A trailing zero counter / leading zero counter.
/// Set MODE to 0 for trailing zero counter => cnt_o is the number of trailing zeros (from the LSB)
/// Set MODE to 1 for leading zero counter  => cnt_o is the number of leading zeros  (from the MSB)
/// If the input does not contain a zero, `empty_o` is asserted. Additionally `cnt_o` contains
/// the maximum number of zeros - 1. For example:
///   in_i = 000_0000, empty_o = 1, cnt_o = 6 (mode = 0)
///   in_i = 000_0001, empty_o = 0, cnt_o = 0 (mode = 0)
///   in_i = 000_1000, empty_o = 0, cnt_o = 3 (mode = 0)
/// Furthermore, this unit contains a more efficient implementation for Verilator (simulation only).
/// This speeds up simulation significantly.
module lzc #(
  /// The width of the input vector.
  parameter int unsigned WIDTH = 2,
  /// Mode selection: 0 -> trailing zero, 1 -> leading zero
  parameter bit          MODE  = 1'b0,
  /// Dependent parameter. Do **not** change!
  ///
  /// Width of the output signal with the zero count.
  parameter int unsigned CNT_WIDTH = cf_math_pkg::idx_width(WIDTH)
) (
  /// Input vector to be counted.
  input  logic [WIDTH-1:0]     in_i,
  /// Count of the leading / trailing zeros.
  output logic [CNT_WIDTH-1:0] cnt_o,
  /// Counter is empty: Asserted if all bits in in_i are zero.
  output logic                 empty_o
);

  if (WIDTH == 1) begin : gen_degenerate_lzc

    assign cnt_o[0] = !in_i[0];
    assign empty_o = !in_i[0];

  end else begin : gen_lzc

    localparam int unsigned NumLevels = cf_math_pkg::clog(WIDTH);


    // index_lut / index_nodes are flat: entry e occupies [e*NumLevels +: NumLevels].
    logic [WIDTH*NumLevels-1:0] index_lut;
    logic [2**NumLevels-1:0] sel_nodes;
    logic [(2**NumLevels)*NumLevels-1:0] index_nodes;

    logic [WIDTH-1:0] in_tmp;

    // reverse vector if required
    always_comb begin : flip_vector
      for (int unsigned i = 0; i < WIDTH; i++) begin
        in_tmp[i] = (MODE) ? in_i[WIDTH-1-i] : in_i[i];
      end
    end

    for (genvar j = 0; $unsigned(j) < WIDTH; j++) begin : g_index_lut
      assign index_lut[(j)*NumLevels +: NumLevels] = ($unsigned(j));
    end

    for (genvar level = 0; $unsigned(level) < NumLevels; level++) begin : g_levels
      if ($unsigned(level) == NumLevels - 1) begin : g_last_level
        for (genvar k = 0; k < 2 ** level; k++) begin : g_level
          // if two successive indices are still in the vector...
          if ($unsigned(k) * 2 < WIDTH - 1) begin : g_reduce
            assign sel_nodes[2 ** level - 1 + k] = in_tmp[k * 2] | in_tmp[k * 2 + 1];
            assign index_nodes[(2 ** level - 1 + k)*NumLevels +: NumLevels] = (in_tmp[k * 2] == 1'b1)
              ? index_lut[(k * 2)*NumLevels +: NumLevels] :
                index_lut[(k * 2 + 1)*NumLevels +: NumLevels];
          end
          // if only the first index is still in the vector...
          if ($unsigned(k) * 2 == WIDTH - 1) begin : g_base
            assign sel_nodes[2 ** level - 1 + k] = in_tmp[k * 2];
            assign index_nodes[(2 ** level - 1 + k)*NumLevels +: NumLevels] = index_lut[(k * 2)*NumLevels +: NumLevels];
          end
          // if index is out of range
          if ($unsigned(k) * 2 > WIDTH - 1) begin : g_out_of_range
            assign sel_nodes[2 ** level - 1 + k] = 1'b0;
            assign index_nodes[(2 ** level - 1 + k)*NumLevels +: NumLevels] = '0;
          end
        end
      end else begin : g_not_last_level
        for (genvar l = 0; l < 2 ** level; l++) begin : g_level
          assign sel_nodes[2 ** level - 1 + l] =
              sel_nodes[2 ** (level + 1) - 1 + l * 2] | sel_nodes[2 ** (level + 1) - 1 + l * 2 + 1];
          assign index_nodes[(2 ** level - 1 + l)*NumLevels +: NumLevels] = (sel_nodes[2 ** (level + 1) - 1 + l * 2] == 1'b1)
            ? index_nodes[(2 ** (level + 1) - 1 + l * 2)*NumLevels +: NumLevels] :
              index_nodes[(2 ** (level + 1) - 1 + l * 2 + 1)*NumLevels +: NumLevels];
        end
      end
    end

    assign cnt_o = NumLevels > $unsigned(0) ? index_nodes[(0)*NumLevels +: NumLevels] : {(cf_math_pkg::clog(WIDTH)) {1'b0}};
    assign empty_o = NumLevels > $unsigned(0) ? ~sel_nodes[0] : ~(|in_i);

  end : gen_lzc

endmodule : lzc

// Mantissa normalization
module mantissa_norm #(
    parameter int unsigned WIDTH = 8,
    parameter int unsigned EXP_WIDTH = 3,
    parameter int unsigned DECIMAL_POINT = 3     // digits before decimal point
)(
    input logic [WIDTH-1:0] operand_i,
    output logic signed [EXP_WIDTH:0] exp_adjust,
    output logic [WIDTH-1:0] result_o
);
    localparam int unsigned LZC_WIDTH = pdpu_pkg::clog2(WIDTH);
    logic [LZC_WIDTH-1:0] leading_zero_count;
    logic lzc_zeroes;
    
    // leading zero count
    lzc #(
        .WIDTH(WIDTH),
        .MODE(1'b1)
    ) u_lzc(
        .in_i(operand_i),
        .cnt_o(leading_zero_count),
        .empty_o(lzc_zeroes)
    );

    // Exponent adjustment
    always_comb begin
        if(lzc_zeroes) begin
            exp_adjust = '0;
        end
        else if(leading_zero_count <= DECIMAL_POINT - 1) begin
            exp_adjust = DECIMAL_POINT - leading_zero_count - 1;
        end
        else begin
            exp_adjust = -$signed(leading_zero_count - DECIMAL_POINT + 1);
        end
    end

    // Mantissa normalization
    barrel_shifter #(
        .WIDTH(WIDTH),
        .SHIFT_WIDTH(LZC_WIDTH),
        .MODE(1'b0)
    ) u_barrel_shifter(
        .operand_i(operand_i),
        .shift_amount(leading_zero_count),
        .result_o(result_o)
    );
endmodule

// Posit decoder
module posit_decoder #(
    parameter int unsigned n = 16,  // word size
    parameter int unsigned es = 1,  // exponent size
    //do not change
    parameter int unsigned nd = pdpu_pkg::clog2(n-1),
    parameter int unsigned EXP_WIDTH = nd+es,       // not include sign bit
    parameter int unsigned MANT_WIDTH = n-es-3      // not include implicit bit
)(
    input logic [n-1:0] operand_i,
    output logic sign_o,
    output logic signed [EXP_WIDTH:0] rg_exp_o,  
    output logic [MANT_WIDTH:0] mant_norm_o
);

    // ---------------
    // Perform two's complement if negative
    // ---------------
    logic sign;
    logic [n-2:0] operand_value;

    assign sign = operand_i[n-1];
    assign operand_value = sign ? (~operand_i[n-2:0]+1) : operand_i[n-2:0];
    
    // ---------------
    // Leading Zero Count
    // ---------------
    logic regS;
    logic [n-2:0] lzc_operand;
    logic [nd-1:0] leading_zero_count;
    logic lzc_zeroes;       // lzc_zeroes = 1 if input is all 0s

    assign regS = operand_value[n-2];
    assign lzc_operand = regS ? (~operand_value) : operand_value;

    lzc #(
        .WIDTH(n-1),
        .MODE(1'b1)    // mode=1 means "counting leading zeroes"
    ) u_lzc(
        .in_i(lzc_operand),
        .cnt_o(leading_zero_count),
        .empty_o(lzc_zeroes)
    );

    logic [nd-1:0] runlength;       // number of consecutive identical bits in regime field
    logic [nd-1:0] regime_bits;     // bitwidth of regime field
    logic signed [nd:0] regime_k;   // value represented by regime field

    assign runlength = lzc_zeroes ? (n-1) : leading_zero_count;
    assign regime_bits = lzc_zeroes ? (n-1) : (leading_zero_count+1);
    assign regime_k = regS ? ({1'b0,runlength-1}) : ({1'b1,~runlength+1});

    // ---------------
    // Left shift regime field
    // ---------------
    logic [n-2:0] op_no_rg;

    barrel_shifter #(
        .WIDTH(n-1),
        .SHIFT_WIDTH(nd),
        .MODE(1'b0)
    ) u_barrel_shifter(
        .operand_i(operand_value),
        .shift_amount(regime_bits),
        .result_o(op_no_rg)
    );

    // ---------------
    // Extract sign
    // ---------------
    assign sign_o = sign;

    // ---------------
    // Extract valid exponent
    // ---------------
    logic [es:0] exp;
    if(es==0) begin
        assign exp = 0;
    end
    else begin
        assign exp = op_no_rg[n-2:n-2-es+1];
    end
    
    assign rg_exp_o = regime_k << es | exp;

    // ---------------
    // Extract mantissa
    // ---------------
    logic implicit_bit;
    
    assign implicit_bit = |operand_i[n-2:0];
    assign mant_norm_o = {implicit_bit, op_no_rg[n-2-es:2]};
endmodule

// Posit encoder
module posit_encoder #(
    parameter int unsigned n = 16,
    parameter int unsigned es = 1,
    parameter int unsigned nd = pdpu_pkg::clog2(n-1),
    parameter int unsigned EXP_WIDTH = nd+es,       // not include sign bit
    parameter int unsigned MANT_WIDTH = n-es-3      // not include implicit bit
) (
    input logic sign_i,
    input logic signed [EXP_WIDTH:0] rg_exp_i,
    input logic [MANT_WIDTH:0] mant_norm_i,

    output logic [n-1:0] result_o
);
    // ---------------
    // Determine whether input is effective 0
    // ---------------
    logic input_not_zero;
    assign input_not_zero = mant_norm_i[MANT_WIDTH];

    // ---------------
    // Compute regime_k and exp
    // ---------------
    logic signed [EXP_WIDTH-es:0] regime_k;
    logic signed [es:0] exp;

    assign regime_k = rg_exp_i[EXP_WIDTH:es];
    if(es==0) begin
        assign exp = 0;
    end
    else begin
        assign exp = rg_exp_i[es-1:0];
    end

    // ---------------
    // initial regime field
    // ---------------
    logic sign_k;
    logic [n-2:0] rg_const;
    logic [n-2:0] regime;
    
    assign sign_k = rg_exp_i[EXP_WIDTH];
    assign rg_const = 1;
    assign regime = sign_k ? rg_const : ~rg_const;

    // ---------------
    // Compute regime bits
    // ---------------
    logic [EXP_WIDTH-es:0] regime_bits;
    
    assign regime_bits = sign_k ? (~regime_k+2) : (regime_k+2);
    
    // ---------------
    // Combine {regime, Exp, Mantissa}
    // ---------------
    localparam int unsigned TEMP_WIDTH = (n-1)+es+MANT_WIDTH;   // regime + exponent + mantissa
    logic [TEMP_WIDTH-1:0] rg_exp_mant;

    if(es==0) begin
        assign rg_exp_mant = {regime, mant_norm_i[MANT_WIDTH-1:0]};
    end
    else begin
        assign rg_exp_mant = {regime, exp[es-1:0], mant_norm_i[MANT_WIDTH-1:0]}; 
    end

    // ---------------
    // Calculate amount of right shift
    // ---------------
    localparam int unsigned MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT = MANT_WIDTH + es + 1;
    localparam int unsigned SHIFT_WIDTH = pdpu_pkg::clog2(MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT+1);
    logic [SHIFT_WIDTH-1:0] shift_amount;
    
    assign shift_amount = (regime_bits>=n) ? MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT : (regime_bits+(MANT_WIDTH+es-n+1));

    // ---------------
    // Right Shift
    // ---------------
    logic [TEMP_WIDTH+MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT-1:0] value_before_shift, value_after_shift;
    
    assign value_before_shift = rg_exp_mant << MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT;
    barrel_shifter #(
        .WIDTH(TEMP_WIDTH+MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT),
        .SHIFT_WIDTH(SHIFT_WIDTH),
        .MODE(1'b1)
    ) u_barrel_shifter(
        .operand_i(value_before_shift),
        .shift_amount(shift_amount),
        .result_o(value_after_shift)
    );

    // ---------------
    // Compute abs_value and rounding_bits
    // ---------------
    logic [n-2:0] value_before_round;
    logic [MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT-1:0] rounding_bits;

    assign {value_before_round,rounding_bits} = value_after_shift[MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT+n-2:0];

    // ---------------
    // Perform rounding (RNE mode is applied by default)
    // ---------------
    logic round_bit;
    logic sticky_bit;
    logic round_value;
    logic [n-2:0] value_after_round;

    assign round_bit = rounding_bits[MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT-1];
    assign sticky_bit = |rounding_bits[MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT-2:0];
    assign round_value = round_bit & (sticky_bit | value_before_round[0]);
    assign value_after_round = value_before_round + round_value;

    // ---------------
    // Output result
    // ---------------
    logic [n-1:0] normal_result;
    assign normal_result = sign_i ? {1'b1,~value_after_round+1} : {1'b0,value_after_round};
    assign result_o = input_not_zero ? normal_result : '0;

endmodule

/*
PDPU equipped with a fine-grained 6-stage
*/

module pdpu_top_pipelined #(
    parameter int unsigned N = 16,                  // dot-product size
    parameter int unsigned n_i = 8,                 // word size
    parameter int unsigned es_i = 2,                // exponent size
    parameter int unsigned n_o = 16,
    parameter int unsigned es_o = 2,
    parameter int unsigned ALIGN_WIDTH = 14         // alignment width
)(
    input logic clk,
    input logic [N*n_i-1:0] operands_a,        // input vector (Va)
    input logic [N*n_i-1:0] operands_b,        // input vector (Vb)
    input logic [n_o-1:0] acc,                      // previous output (acc)
    output logic [n_o-1:0] result_o                 // output result (out)
);

    // Pipeline 0: Input Reg
    // ********************
    // group_path -name pipe0to1 -from {operands_a operands_b operands_c} -to {pipe1*}

    // Posit Decoding
    // ---------------

    localparam int unsigned EXP_WIDTH_I = pdpu_pkg::clog2(n_i-1) + es_i;
    // the maximum mantissa size (not include implicit bit)

    logic [N*(MANT_WIDTH_I+1)-1:0] mants_norm_a, mants_norm_b;

                .sign_o(signs_a[i]),

                .mant_norm_o(mants_norm_a[(i)*(MANT_WIDTH_I+1) +: (MANT_WIDTH_I+1)])
            );

            posit_decoder #(
                .n(n_i),

                .n(n_i),

    localparam int unsigned EXP_WIDTH_O = pdpu_pkg::clog2(n_o-1) + es_o;
    localparam int unsigned MANT_WIDTH_O = n_o-es_o-3;

    // Extract valid sign, exponent, and mantissa from previous output (acc)

        .sign_o(sign_acc),

        .mant_norm_o(mant_norm_acc)
    );

    // ---------------
    // Sign Process
    // ---------------
    logic [N-1:0] signs_ab;
    logic [N:0] signs;
    
    // Perform XOR to obtain the signs of the product of Va and Vb
    assign signs_ab = signs_a ^ signs_b;
    assign signs = {sign_acc, signs_ab};

    // ---------------
    // Exponent Addition
    // ---------------
    localparam int unsigned EXP_WIDTH = pdpu_pkg::maximum(EXP_WIDTH_I+1,EXP_WIDTH_O);
    
    // obtain the exponents of the product of Va and Vb through signed addition
    logic signed [N*(EXP_WIDTH+1)-1:0] rg_exp_c;
    generate
        genvar m;
        for(m=0;m<N;m++) begin
            assign rg_exp_c[(m)*(EXP_WIDTH+1) +: (EXP_WIDTH+1)] = $signed(rg_exp_a[(m)*(EXP_WIDTH_I+1) +: (EXP_WIDTH_I+1)]) + $signed(rg_exp_b[(m)*(EXP_WIDTH_I+1) +: (EXP_WIDTH_I+1)]);
        end
    endgenerate

    // ********************
    // Pipeline 1
    // ********************
    // group_path -name pipe1to2 -from {pipe1*} -to {pipe2*}
    logic [N:0] pipe1_signs;
    logic signed [N*(EXP_WIDTH+1)-1:0] pipe1_rg_exp_c;
    logic [N*(MANT_WIDTH_I+1)-1:0] pipe1_mants_norm_a, pipe1_mants_norm_b;
    logic signed [EXP_WIDTH_O:0] pipe1_rg_exp_acc;
    logic [MANT_WIDTH_O:0] pipe1_mant_norm_acc;

    `FFNR(pipe1_signs,signs,clk)
    `FFNR(pipe1_rg_exp_c,rg_exp_c,clk)
    `FFNR(pipe1_mants_norm_a,mants_norm_a,clk)
    `FFNR(pipe1_mants_norm_b,mants_norm_b,clk)
    `FFNR(pipe1_rg_exp_acc,rg_exp_acc,clk)
    `FFNR(pipe1_mant_norm_acc,mant_norm_acc,clk)

    // ---------------
    // Mantissa Multiplication
    // ---------------
    localparam int unsigned MUL_WIDTH = 2*(MANT_WIDTH_I+1);
    logic [N*MUL_WIDTH-1:0] mul_sum, mul_carry;

    // Mantissa multiplication is performed by a modified radix-4 booth multiplier
    generate
        genvar j;
        for(j=0;j<N;j++) begin: multiplication
            radix4_booth_multiplier #(
                .WIDTH_A(MANT_WIDTH_I+1),
                .WIDTH_B(MANT_WIDTH_I+1)
            ) u_radix4_booth_multiplier(
                .operand_a(pipe1_mants_norm_a[(j)*(MANT_WIDTH_I+1) +: (MANT_WIDTH_I+1)]),
                .operand_b(pipe1_mants_norm_b[(j)*(MANT_WIDTH_I+1) +: (MANT_WIDTH_I+1)]),
                .sum_o(mul_sum[(j)*MUL_WIDTH +: MUL_WIDTH]),
                .carry_o(mul_carry[(j)*MUL_WIDTH +: MUL_WIDTH])
            );
        end
    endgenerate

    // ---------------
    // Exponent Compare
    // ---------------
    logic signed [(N+1)*(EXP_WIDTH+1)-1:0] rg_exp_items;
    generate
        genvar u;
        for(u=0;u<N;u++) begin
            assign rg_exp_items[(u)*(EXP_WIDTH+1) +: (EXP_WIDTH+1)] = pipe1_rg_exp_c[(u)*(EXP_WIDTH+1) +: (EXP_WIDTH+1)];
        end
    endgenerate
    assign rg_exp_items[(N)*(EXP_WIDTH+1) +: (EXP_WIDTH+1)] = $signed(pipe1_rg_exp_acc);

    // Obtain the maximum exponent by a recursive comparator tree
    logic signed [EXP_WIDTH:0] rg_exp_max;
    comp_tree #(
        .N(N+1),
        .WIDTH(EXP_WIDTH)
    ) u_comp_tree(
        .operands_i(rg_exp_items),
        .result_o(rg_exp_max)
    );

    // *******************
    // Pipeline 2
    // *******************
    // group_path -name pipe2to3 -from {pipe2*} -to {pipe3*}
    logic [N:0] pipe2_signs;
    logic signed [(N+1)*(EXP_WIDTH+1)-1:0] pipe2_rg_exp_items;
    logic signed [EXP_WIDTH:0] pipe2_rg_exp_max;
    logic [N*MUL_WIDTH-1:0] pipe2_mul_sum, pipe2_mul_carry;
    
    logic [MANT_WIDTH_O:0] pipe2_mant_norm_acc;


    `FFNR(pipe2_signs,pipe1_signs,clk)
    `FFNR(pipe2_rg_exp_items,rg_exp_items,clk)
    `FFNR(pipe2_rg_exp_max,rg_exp_max,clk)
    `FFNR(pipe2_mul_sum,mul_sum,clk)
    `FFNR(pipe2_mul_carry,mul_carry,clk)
    
    `FFNR(pipe2_mant_norm_acc,pipe1_mant_norm_acc,clk)

    // ---------------
    // The final addition
    // ---------------
    logic [N*MUL_WIDTH-1:0] mants_norm_c;
    generate
        genvar v;
        for(v=0;v<N;v++) begin
            assign mants_norm_c[(v)*MUL_WIDTH +: MUL_WIDTH] = pipe2_mul_sum[(v)*MUL_WIDTH +: MUL_WIDTH] + pipe2_mul_carry[(v)*MUL_WIDTH +: MUL_WIDTH];
        end
    endgenerate

    // ---------------
    // Mantissa Alignment
    // ---------------
    logic [(N+1)*ALIGN_WIDTH-1:0] product;
    logic [(N+1)*ALIGN_WIDTH-1:0] product_shifted;

    // the mantissa products of Va and Vb are kept in values of bitwidth `ALIGN_WIDTH` before alignment
    generate
        genvar k;
        if(ALIGN_WIDTH>MUL_WIDTH) begin: fixed_shift
            for(k=0;k<N;k++) begin
                assign product[(k)*ALIGN_WIDTH +: ALIGN_WIDTH] = mants_norm_c[(k)*MUL_WIDTH +: MUL_WIDTH] << (ALIGN_WIDTH-MUL_WIDTH);
            end
        end  
        else begin: fixed_shift
            for(k=0;k<N;k++) begin
                assign product[(k)*ALIGN_WIDTH +: ALIGN_WIDTH] = mants_norm_c[(k)*MUL_WIDTH +: MUL_WIDTH] >> (MUL_WIDTH-ALIGN_WIDTH);
            end
        end
    endgenerate

    if(ALIGN_WIDTH>MANT_WIDTH_O+2) begin
        assign product[(N)*ALIGN_WIDTH +: ALIGN_WIDTH] = pipe2_mant_norm_acc << (ALIGN_WIDTH-MANT_WIDTH_O-2);
    end
    else begin
        assign product[(N)*ALIGN_WIDTH +: ALIGN_WIDTH] = pipe2_mant_norm_acc >> (MANT_WIDTH_O+2-ALIGN_WIDTH);
    end

    // Align the products according to the difference between the respective exponent and the maximum exponent
    localparam int unsigned SHIFT_WIDTH = pdpu_pkg::clog2(ALIGN_WIDTH+1);
    logic [(N+1)*(EXP_WIDTH+1)-1:0] rg_exp_diff;
    logic [(N+1)*SHIFT_WIDTH-1:0] shift_amount;
    generate
        genvar z,s;
        // compute the respective exponent difference
        for(z=0;z<N+1;z++) begin
            assign rg_exp_diff[(z)*(EXP_WIDTH+1) +: (EXP_WIDTH+1)] = $unsigned(pipe2_rg_exp_max - pipe2_rg_exp_items[(z)*(EXP_WIDTH+1) +: (EXP_WIDTH+1)]);
        end
        // the maximum shift amount is ALIGN_WIDTH, since the bits exceeding ALIGN_WIDTH will be discarded directly.
        if(EXP_WIDTH+1>SHIFT_WIDTH) begin
            for(s=0;s<N+1;s++) begin
                assign shift_amount[(s)*SHIFT_WIDTH +: SHIFT_WIDTH] = (|rg_exp_diff[(s)*(EXP_WIDTH+1) + SHIFT_WIDTH +: (EXP_WIDTH-SHIFT_WIDTH+1)]) ? ALIGN_WIDTH : rg_exp_diff[(s)*(EXP_WIDTH+1) +: SHIFT_WIDTH];
                barrel_shifter #(
                    .WIDTH(ALIGN_WIDTH),
                    .SHIFT_WIDTH(SHIFT_WIDTH),
                    .MODE(1'b1)
                ) u_barrel_shifter(
                    .operand_i(product[(s)*ALIGN_WIDTH +: ALIGN_WIDTH]),
                    .shift_amount(shift_amount[(s)*SHIFT_WIDTH +: SHIFT_WIDTH]),
                    .result_o(product_shifted[(s)*ALIGN_WIDTH +: ALIGN_WIDTH])
                );
            end
        end

        else begin
            for(s=0;s<N+1;s++) begin
                barrel_shifter #(
                    .WIDTH(ALIGN_WIDTH),
                    .SHIFT_WIDTH(EXP_WIDTH+1),
                    .MODE(1'b1)
                ) u_barrel_shifter(
                    .operand_i(product[(s)*ALIGN_WIDTH +: ALIGN_WIDTH]),
                    .shift_amount(rg_exp_diff[(s)*(EXP_WIDTH+1) +: (EXP_WIDTH+1)]),
                    .result_o(product_shifted[(s)*ALIGN_WIDTH +: ALIGN_WIDTH])
                );
            end
        end
    endgenerate

    // ---------------
    // Mantissa accumulation in two's complement
    // ---------------
    localparam int unsigned CARRY_WIDTH = pdpu_pkg::clog2(N+1);
    localparam int unsigned SUM_WIDTH = ALIGN_WIDTH + CARRY_WIDTH;

    logic [(N+1)*(SUM_WIDTH+1)-1:0] mantissa, mantissa_comp;
    // Convert to two's complement
    generate
        genvar y;
        for(y=0;y<N+1;y++) begin
            assign mantissa[(y)*(SUM_WIDTH+1) +: (SUM_WIDTH+1)] = product_shifted[(y)*ALIGN_WIDTH +: ALIGN_WIDTH];
            assign mantissa_comp[(y)*(SUM_WIDTH+1) +: (SUM_WIDTH+1)] = pipe2_signs[y] ? (~mantissa[(y)*(SUM_WIDTH+1) +: (SUM_WIDTH+1)]+1) : mantissa[(y)*(SUM_WIDTH+1) +: (SUM_WIDTH+1)];
        end
    endgenerate

    // *******************
    // Pipeline 3
    // *******************
    // group_path -name pipe3to4 -from {pipe3*} -to {pipe4*}
    logic signed [EXP_WIDTH:0] pipe3_rg_exp_max;
    logic [(N+1)*(SUM_WIDTH+1)-1:0] pipe3_mantissa_comp;

    `FFNR(pipe3_rg_exp_max,pipe2_rg_exp_max,clk)
    `FFNR(pipe3_mantissa_comp,mantissa_comp,clk)

    logic [SUM_WIDTH:0] csa_sum,csa_carry;
    csa_tree #(
        .N(N+1),
        .WIDTH_I(SUM_WIDTH+1),
        .WIDTH_O(SUM_WIDTH+1)
    ) u_csa_tree(
        .operands_i(pipe3_mantissa_comp),
        .sum_o(csa_sum),
        .carry_o(csa_carry)
    );

    logic [SUM_WIDTH:0] sum_result;
    // the final addition
    assign sum_result = csa_sum + csa_carry;


    // ********************
    // Pipeline 4
    // ********************
    // group_path -name pipe4to5 -from {pipe4*} -to {pipe5*}
    logic [SUM_WIDTH:0] pipe4_sum_result;
    logic signed [EXP_WIDTH:0] pipe4_rg_exp_max;

    `FFNR(pipe4_sum_result,sum_result,clk)
    `FFNR(pipe4_rg_exp_max,pipe3_rg_exp_max,clk)


    logic final_sign;
    logic [SUM_WIDTH-1:0] sum_c;
    
    assign final_sign = pipe4_sum_result[SUM_WIDTH];
    assign sum_c = final_sign ? (~pipe4_sum_result+1) : pipe4_sum_result[SUM_WIDTH-1:0];

    // ---------------
    // Mantissa normalization and exponent adjustment
    // ---------------
    logic signed [EXP_WIDTH:0] rg_exp_adjust;
    logic signed [EXP_WIDTH:0] final_rg_exp;
    logic [SUM_WIDTH-1:0] sum_norm;

    mantissa_norm #(
        .WIDTH(SUM_WIDTH),
        .EXP_WIDTH(EXP_WIDTH),
        .DECIMAL_POINT(CARRY_WIDTH+2)
    ) u_mantissa_norm(
        .operand_i(sum_c),
        .exp_adjust(rg_exp_adjust),
        .result_o(sum_norm)
    );
    // Note: is there any possibility of overflow?
    assign final_rg_exp = pipe4_rg_exp_max + rg_exp_adjust;

    // ---------------
    // Posit encoder and Rounding
    // ---------------
    logic [MANT_WIDTH_O+2:0] final_mant;
    
    if(SUM_WIDTH>MANT_WIDTH_O+3) begin
        logic [SUM_WIDTH-MANT_WIDTH_O-3:0] sticky_bits;
        logic sticky_bit;
        assign sticky_bits = sum_norm[SUM_WIDTH-MANT_WIDTH_O-3:0];
        assign sticky_bit = |sticky_bits;
        assign final_mant = {sum_norm[SUM_WIDTH-1:SUM_WIDTH-MANT_WIDTH_O-2],sticky_bit};
    end
    else begin
        assign final_mant = sum_norm << (MANT_WIDTH_O+3-SUM_WIDTH);
    end


    // ********************
    // Pipeline 5
    // ********************
    // group_path -name pipe5to6 -from {pipe5*} -to {result_o}
    logic pipe5_final_sign;
    logic signed [EXP_WIDTH:0] pipe5_final_rg_exp;
    logic [MANT_WIDTH_O+2:0] pipe5_final_mant;

    `FFNR(pipe5_final_sign,final_sign,clk)
    `FFNR(pipe5_final_rg_exp,final_rg_exp,clk)
    `FFNR(pipe5_final_mant,final_mant,clk)


    posit_encoder #(
        .n(n_o),
        .es(es_o),
        .EXP_WIDTH(EXP_WIDTH),
        .MANT_WIDTH(MANT_WIDTH_O+2)
    ) u_posit_encoder(
        .sign_i(pipe5_final_sign),
        .rg_exp_i(pipe5_final_rg_exp),
        .mant_norm_i(pipe5_final_mant),
        .result_o(result_o)
    );


    // ********************
    // Pipeline 6: Output Reg
    // ********************
    // No group_path

Now I have the full picture. Let me analyze the pipeline stages:

- **Stage 0→1**: Posit decoding, sign XOR, exponent addition → registered into pipe1
- **Stage 1→2**: Booth multiplier, exponent compare (comp_tree for max) → registered into pipe2
- **Stage 2→3**: mul_sum+carry addition, alignment (shift), two's complement → registered into pipe3 (CRITICAL PATH)
- **Stage 3→4**: CSA tree, final add → registered into pipe4
- **Stage 4→5**: sign extraction, mantissa normalization (LZC+shift), posit encoder setup → registered into pipe5
- **Stage 5→output**: posit encoder

The critical path (pipe2→pipe3) includes: mul_sum+carry add, left-shift to ALIGN_WIDTH, exp_diff computation, shift_amount clamping, barrel shift (right), and two's complement. The path starts from pipe2_rg_exp_max.

**Key optimization**: The two's complement (`~mantissa + 1`) in stage 2→3 is on the critical path. I can replace it with ones'-complement + carry injection into the CSA tree in stage 3. Instead of computing `mantissa_comp = sign ? ~mantissa + 1 : mantissa`, I can:
1. Pass `sign ? ~mantissa : mantissa` (ones' complement, no +1 adder)
2. Add a carry-in bit into the CSA tree equal to the sign (since `~x + 1 = -x` in two's complement)

This eliminates the carry propagation adder from the critical path. The CSA tree already absorbs the extra carry-in for free.

But wait - the CSA tree takes N+1=17 operands, each SUM_WIDTH+1=20 bits. Adding a carry-in per signed operand means I need to inject 17 carry bits. Since the CSA tree compresses to sum+carry, I can add these carry bits as an extra input column (bit 0 of each operand gets +1 when sign is set).

Since the file is huge, let me apply targeted edits to the starting_point.v and evaluate that file. But I need to preserve the original. Let me create design.sv as a copy using a trick - I'll read the file and write it out.

Actually, I can just apply edits to `starting_point.v` directly and run evaluation on it. The original content is preserved in my conversation history. Let me start optimizing.

**Optimization 1**: Move the two's complement `+1` carry out of the critical path by injecting it into the CSA tree.

[ASSISTANT]
Now I understand the full structure. Let me implement my first optimization. The key idea:

Instead of `mantissa_comp = signs[y] ? ~mantissa + 1 : mantissa`, I'll use:
- `mantissa_comp = signs[y] ? ~mantissa : mantissa` (ones' complement, no adder)
- The `+1` for each negative operand gets injected as an extra carry into the CSA tree

The CSA tree takes 17 operands of 20 bits each. To inject the carry-in for the `+1` of each two's complement, I need to add `signs[y]` at bit position 0 of operand y. The simplest way: create an 18th operand that's just the sign bits packed at the bottom. But the CSA tree has fixed N. 

A cleaner approach: since `~x + 1 = -x`, and we're summing all operands, the sum of all the `+1`s from negatives is just `popcount(signs)`. This is a constant for the cycle. I can add it to the final `sum_result = csa_sum + csa_carry + popcount(signs)`.

Wait, but that's not quite right. The `+1` is per-operand at bit 0. If I fold them all into the final add, `sum_result = csa_sum + csa_carry + popcount(signs)` works because:
- Original: sum of (signs[y] ? ~mant[y]+1 : mant[y]) = sum of (signs[y] ? ~mant[y] : mant[y]) + sum of signs[y]
- The ones' complement sum goes through the CSA tree, and the total `+popcount(signs)` can be added at the end.

But `popcount(signs)` is at most 17, which fits in 5 bits. Adding it to `sum_result` (which is SUM_WIDTH+1 = 20 bits) is cheap.

Actually wait - the CSA tree operates on the ones'-complement values. The CSA tree compresses them to csa_sum + csa_carry. Then `sum_result = csa_sum + csa_carry + popcount(signs)`. This is correct!

Let me implement this. I'll modify the mantissa_comp to use ones' complement, and add the popcount to sum_result. I also need to pipe the signs through to stage 3→4 (for the popcount addition).

Actually, the popcount needs to be available in the same stage as sum_result computation (stage 3→4). The signs are in pipe2_signs. I need to compute popcount from pipe2_signs and register it, or compute it in stage 3.

Wait, `sum_result` is computed in stage 3 (after pipe3 registers). The signs are in pipe2_signs, which get registered into... hmm, pipe2_signs is not directly piped to stage 3. Let me check: pipe3_mantissa_comp is registered, and pipe3_rg_exp_max is registered. The signs are not explicitly piped to stage 3.

Actually, looking more carefully: the signs (pipe2_signs) are used to compute mantissa_comp in stage 2, and mantissa_comp is registered into pipe3. So the signs are "consumed" in stage 2. For my optimization, I need the popcount in stage 3 (where sum_result is computed). So I need to pipe the popcount through a register.

Let me compute `signs_popcount = $unsigned($signed(pipe2_signs))` (or rather `$countones(pipe2_signs)`) in stage 2, register it into pipe3, and add it to sum_result in stage 3.

Actually, `$countones` might not synthesize well. Let me use an adder tree. With 17 bits, I can build a popcount adder tree. Or I can simply add the signs one by one. Since this is a 17-bit popcount producing a 5-bit result, it's relatively small.

Let me also think about whether there's an even simpler approach. The signs pipe2_signs is already registered. Computing popcount from it is combinational and then I register it. The popcount of 17 bits → 5 bits. An adder tree of depth 4-5. This is much smaller than the 17 full 20-bit adders I'm removing from the critical path.

Actually, even simpler: I can fold the `+1` carries into the CSA tree directly by extending each operand by 1 bit at the bottom. But that changes the CSA tree interface. The simplest correct approach is the popcount approach.

Let me make the changes to `starting_point.v`:
  -> edit_file(filename='starting_point.v', old_string="    logic [(N+1)*(SUM_WIDTH+1)-1:0] mantissa, mantissa_comp;\n    // Convert to two's complement\n    generate\n        genvar y;\n        for(y=0;y<N+1;y++) begin\n            assign mantissa[(y)*(SUM_WIDTH+1) +: (SUM_WIDTH+1)] = product_shifted[(y)*ALIGN_WIDTH +: ALIGN_WIDTH];\n            assign mantissa_comp[(y)*(SUM_WIDTH+1) +: (SUM_WIDTH+1)] = pipe2_signs[y] ? (~mantissa[(y)*(SUM_WIDTH+1) +: (SUM_WIDTH+1)]+1) : mantissa[(y)*(SUM_WIDTH+1) +: (SUM_WIDTH+1)];\n        end\n    endgenerate", new_string="    logic [(N+1)*(SUM_WIDTH+1)-1:0] mantissa, mantissa_comp;\n    logic [CARRY_WIDTH:0] signs_popcount;\n    // Convert to ones' complement (carry-in for two's complement added later)\n    generate\n        genvar y;\n        for(y=0;y<N+1;y++) begin\n            assign mantissa[(y)*(SUM_WIDTH+1) +: (SUM_WIDTH+1)] = product_shifted[(y)*ALIGN_WIDTH +: ALIGN_WIDTH];\n            assign mantissa_comp[(y)*(SUM_WIDTH+1) +: (SUM_WIDTH+1)] = pipe2_signs[y] ? ~mantissa[(y)*(SUM_WIDTH+1) +: (SUM_WIDTH+1)] : mantissa[(y)*(SUM_WIDTH+1) +: (SUM_WIDTH+1)];\n        end\n    endgenerate\n    // Popcount of signs = total carry-in for two's complement restoration\n    always_comb begin\n        signs_popcount = '0;\n        for (int y = 0; y < N+1; y++)\n            signs_popcount = signs_popcount + pipe2_signs[y];\n    end")

// 5:3 counter, the basic module that constitutes the 4:2 compressor
module counter_5to3(
    input logic x1,x2,x3,x4,cin,
    output logic sum,carry,cout
);
    assign sum = x1 ^ x2 ^ x3 ^ x4 ^ cin;

[ASSISTANT]
Cost dropped from 855919 to 817669 (total 15.5% reduction from original). The critical path is still pipe3→pipe4 (CSA tree + final add). The path shows XOR/XNOR chains (the CSA tree compressors) followed by the final add.

The CSA tree for 18 operands of 20 bits has depth: 18 → split 9+9 → 4:2 compress → 4+5 and 4+5 → compress → etc. The compressor_4to2 has a carry chain (cin/cout between bits), which creates the XOR/XNOR chain.

Looking at the critical path more carefully: the first 19 gates are XOR/XNOR (the 4:2 compressor carry chain), then more complex gates. The 4:2 compressor's counter_5to3 has a carry chain (cout → next cin). For 20-bit width, this creates a 20-deep carry chain!

**Optimization 4**: The 4:2 compressor's internal carry chain is the bottleneck. The counter_5to3 module has `cout` feeding into the next `cin`, creating a ripple chain. I can break this by making the 4:2 compressor use parallel carry instead.

The standard 4:2 compressor uses two cascaded full adders with a horizontal carry. But the carry-out should be a separate signal, not chained. Let me look at the counter_5to3:

```
assign sum = x1 ^ x2 ^ x3 ^ x4 ^ cin;
assign cout = (x1 ^ x2) & x3 | ~(x1 ^ x2) & x1;
assign carry = (x1 ^ x2 ^ x3 ^ x4) & cin | ~(x1 ^ x2 ^ x3 ^ x4) & x4;
```

Here, `cout` depends only on x1,x2,x3,x4 (not cin), and `carry` depends on cin. The chain is `cout[i] → cin[i+1] → carry[i+1]`. But `cout` doesn't depend on `cin`, so the chain is: cout[0] → carry[1] (depends on cin[1]=cout[0]) → cout[1] (independent of cin[1]) → carry[2] (depends on cin[2]=cout[1])...

Wait, `cout` doesn't depend on `cin` at all! So the chain is: carry[i] depends on cin[i] = cout[i-1], and cout[i-1] depends only on inputs. So the depth is: cout[0] → carry[1] → (cout[1] is independent) → carry[2] depends on cout[1]... 

Actually the carry chain is: carry[i] depends on cin[i]=cout[i-1], and cout[i-1] depends on x1,x2,x3,x4 (no cin). So the delay for carry[i] is: delay(cout[i-1]) + delay(carry[i] computation). And carry[i] feeds into the output carry_o. But carry[i] also doesn't feed cout[i+1]. So the chain is just 1-deep per stage? No...

Let me re-examine. The 4:2 compressor:
```
assign cin[0] = 1'b0;
for i in 0..WIDTH_I-1:
    cin[i+1] = cout[i]  // chain!
    sum[i] = x1^x2^x3^x4^cin[i]  // depends on cin[i]
    carry[i] = (...) & cin[i] | (...) & x4  // depends on cin[i]
    cout[i] = (x1^x2)&x3 | ~(x1^x2)&x1  // independent of cin[i]
```

So `cout[i]` is independent of `cin[i]`, meaning `cout[i]` is computed in parallel. But `cin[i+1] = cout[i]` and `sum[i]` depends on `cin[i]`. The sum bits form a chain through the carry: sum[1] depends on cin[1]=cout[0], sum[2] depends on cin[2]=cout[1], etc. But cout is computed independently, so the delay is just one gate for all couts, then one gate for all sums. So the 4:2 compressor should be fast (2-deep).

The critical path shows 19 XOR/XNOR gates in sequence. That seems like the CSA tree structure itself, not a single compressor. The recursive csa_tree splits 18→9+9→4:2 compress, then 9→4+5→4:2 compress, etc. The issue is that the recursive structure creates serial dependencies.

Actually, looking at the path again: the first 19 gates are all XOR/XNOR, suggesting a long XOR chain. This could be the sum path through multiple levels of CSA tree. Each 4:2 compressor produces sum bits that feed into the next level.

Let me try a different approach: replace the recursive CSA tree with a simpler, flatter structure. For 18 operands, I can use a simple tree of 3:2 compressors (full adders) in a Wallace tree structure. Or I can just let the synthesis tool optimize the sum directly.

Actually, let me try replacing the CSA tree with a simple `always_comb` sum of all operands. The synthesis tool might handle it better:
  -> edit_file(filename='starting_point.v', new_string="    logic [SUM_WIDTH:0] csa_sum,csa_carry;\n    // CSA tree compresses the ones'-complement mantissas plus the popcount\n    // of signs (as an extra operand) to restore two's-complement carry-ins.\n    logic [(N+2)*(SUM_WIDTH+1)-1:0] csa_operands;\n    assign csa_operands = {{(SUM_WIDTH-CARRY_WIDTH){1'b0}}, pipe3_signs_popcount, pipe3_mantissa_comp};\n    // Flatten: directly accumulate all operands in ones' complement + popcount\n    logic [SUM_WIDTH:0] sum_result;\n    always_comb begin\n        sum_result = '0;\n        for (int y = 0; y < N+1; y++)\n            sum_result = sum_result + $signed(pipe3_mantissa_comp[y*(SUM_WIDTH+1) +: (SUM_WIDTH+1)]);\n        sum_result = sum_result + pipe3_signs_popcount;\n    end", old_string="    logic [SUM_WIDTH:0] csa_sum,csa_carry;\n    // CSA tree compresses the ones'-complement mantissas plus the popcount\n    // of signs (as an extra operand) to restore two's-complement carry-ins.\n    logic [(N+2)*(SUM_WIDTH+1)-1:0] csa_operands;\n    assign csa_operands = {{(SUM_WIDTH-CARRY_WIDTH){1'b0}}, pipe3_signs_popcount, pipe3_mantissa_comp};\n    csa_tree #(\n        .N(N+2),\n        .WIDTH_I(SUM_WIDTH+1),\n        .WIDTH_O(SUM_WIDTH+1)\n    ) u_csa_tree(\n        .operands_i(csa_operands),\n        .sum_o(csa_sum),\n        .carry_o(csa_carry)\n    );\n\n    logic [SUM_WIDTH:0] sum_result;\n    // the final addition (only two operands now)\n    assign sum_result = csa_sum + csa_carry;")

[ASSISTANT]
Cost dropped to 795922 (total 17.8% reduction from original). The critical path moved to stage 5→output: the posit_encoder (784ps). The simple for-loop sum is actually better than the recursive CSA tree!

Now the bottleneck is the posit_encoder in the last stage. Let me look at what the posit_encoder does and how to optimize it.

The posit_encoder takes sign, rg_exp, and mantissa, and encodes them into a posit(16,2). It involves:
1. Extract regime_k and exp from rg_exp
2. Build regime field
3. Compute regime_bits and shift_amount
4. Barrel shift right
5. Rounding (round_bit, sticky_bit, round_value, add)
6. Two's complement for negative result

The critical path starts from pipe5_final_rg_exp, goes through the shift_amount computation, barrel shift, rounding, and output. The NOR4 gate (86.67ps) early in the path suggests the regime_bits computation or shift_amount.

Let me look at the barrel shifter in the encoder - it shifts by `shift_amount` which is up to MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT = MANT_WIDTH + es + 1 = 13 + 2 + 1 = 16. The SHIFT_WIDTH = clog2(17) = 5. But the barrel_shifter module uses a generate with SHIFT_WIDTH stages, each shifting by powers of 2.

One issue: the barrel shifter uses `temp_results` which is a flat array indexed by `i` and `i-1`. For SHIFT_WIDTH=5, this creates 5 stages of muxes. The first stage shifts by 16, then 8, 4, 2, 1. This is a standard log-depth barrel shifter.

But the critical path goes through the barrel shifter AND the rounding AND the two's complement. Let me see if I can optimize the posit encoder.

Actually, let me look at the barrel_shifter module more carefully. There's a potential issue with how `temp_results` is declared:

```
logic [SHIFT_WIDTH-1:0][WIDTH-1:0] temp_results;
```

This is a 2D packed array. The generate block accesses `temp_results[i]` and `temp_results[i-1]`. For the synthesis tool, this should be fine.

Let me think about what else I can optimize. The barrel shifter in the encoder has WIDTH = TEMP_WIDTH + MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT = 16 + 13 + 2 + 16 = ... let me compute: TEMP_WIDTH = (n-1) + es + MANT_WIDTH = 15 + 2 + 13 = 30. MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT = MANT_WIDTH + es + 1 = 13 + 2 + 1 = 16. So the barrel shifter width is 30 + 16 = 46 bits, with SHIFT_WIDTH = clog2(17) = 5 bits.

That's a 46-bit barrel shifter with 5 stages. Each stage has a 46-bit mux. This is significant.

**Optimization 5**: Simplify the barrel shifter. The current barrel_shifter uses a generate loop with a specific structure. Let me try replacing it with a simpler direct implementation that the synthesis tool can optimize better.

Actually, looking at the barrel_shifter code:
```
assign temp_results[SHIFT_WIDTH-1] = operand_i;
for(i=SHIFT_WIDTH-1;i>0;i--)
    temp_results[i-1] = shift_amount[i] ? temp_results[i]<<(2**i) : temp_results[i];
result_o = shift_amount[0] ? temp_results[0]<<1 : temp_results;
```

Wait, the result_o assignment references `temp_results` without an index! That should be `temp_results[0]`. This might be a bug in the original code, but since it passes simulation, it must be getting interpreted as `temp_results[0]` by some tools. Let me check...

Actually, `temp_results` without an index in Verilog would be the entire packed array, which is a wider value. But since result_o is WIDTH bits and temp_results is SHIFT_WIDTH*WIDTH bits, the lower bits would be used. And `temp_results[0]` occupies the lowest WIDTH bits. So `temp_results` and `temp_results[0]` have the same lower bits! So it works by accident.

Let me try replacing the barrel_shifter with a simpler version:

Actually, the recursive comp_tree structure already forms a balanced-ish tree. The issue might be that the comparator module is still slow. Let me look at what exactly is happening:

The comparator does `result_o = (operand_a >= operand_b) ? operand_a : operand_b`. This is a signed comparison + 2:1 mux. For 8-bit signed values, the comparison involves looking at sign bits and magnitude.

An alternative: instead of a tree of comparators that each pass the larger value, I could compute the max index first (smaller comparison, no muxing of full values), then select. But that would need an extra mux stage.

One key optimization: the posit encoder's barrel shifter is 46 bits wide with 5 stages. The critical path goes through this shifter. Let me see if I can reduce the barrel shifter width.

Looking at the encoder:
- `value_before_shift = rg_exp_mant << MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT` (left-shift by 16)
- Then barrel shift right by `shift_amount` (0..16)
- Extract `value_before_round` (15 bits) and `rounding_bits` (16 bits) from `value_after_shift`

The total width is TEMP_WIDTH + MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT = 30 + 16 = 46. But after the left shift by 16, the lower 16 bits are always 0. And after the right shift, we only use bits [31:0] (16+15=31). So we could reduce the barrel shifter width.

Actually, let me think about this differently. The value_before_shift = rg_exp_mant << 16. This means bits [15:0] are 0, and bits [45:16] = rg_exp_mant[29:0]. The barrel shifter shifts right by shift_amount (0..16), producing value_after_shift. We then take bits [31:0] (value_before_round[15:0] = bits[31:16], rounding_bits[16:0] = bits[15:0]).

Since the lower 16 bits of value_before_shift are 0, after a right shift by s, bits [15:0] come from the zeros (if s≥0) or from rg_exp_mant. For s=0, bits[15:0]=0, bits[31:16]=rg_exp_mant[15:0]. For s=16, bits[15:0]=rg_exp_mant[15:0], bits[31:16]=rg_exp_mant[29:14].

So the effective computation is: take rg_exp_mant (30 bits), right-shift by shift_amount, and take the top 16 bits as value_before_round and the next 16 bits as rounding_bits. But since rg_exp_mant is only 30 bits, we can simplify.

Actually, this is equivalent to: `{value_before_round, rounding_bits} = rg_exp_mant << (16 - shift_amount)` but with zero-padding. Since rg_exp_mant is 30 bits and the output is 32 bits, when shift_amount=0, we get rg_exp_mant << 16 (which is 46 bits, but we take 32). When shift_amount=16, we get rg_exp_mant << 0 = rg_exp_mant (30 bits, zero-extended to 32).

So `value_after_shift[31:0] = rg_exp_mant << (16 - shift_amount)` with appropriate zero-extension. This is a left barrel shift of rg_exp_mant by (16-shift_amount) = 0..16, taking 32 bits.

I see the barrel shifter is 46 bits wide (TEMP_WIDTH=30 + MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT=16). The left shift by 16 means the lower 16 bits are always 0. Then we shift right by shift_amount (0..16). The result we use is bits [31:0] = {value_before_round[15:0], rounding_bits[15:0]}.

A simpler optimization: since the lower 16 bits of `value_before_shift` are zero, the barrel shifter's right shift by `shift_amount` means:
- result = {0[16 bits], rg_exp_mant[30 bits]} >> shift_amount
- We use bits [31:0] of the result

For shift_amount=0: result[31:0] = {rg_exp_mant[15:0], 16'b0}

Actually, the issue is that the barrel shifter is 46 bits wide and has 5 stages (SHIFT_WIDTH=5 for MAX_SHIFT_AMOUNT+1=17). Each stage processes 46 bits. Let me try reducing it to 32 bits:

Since we only use bits [31:0] of value_after_shift, and the upper bits (32..45) are only from rg_exp_mant[30:16] when shift_amount < 16, we can safely ignore them. So we can use a 32-bit barrel shifter on the lower 32 bits of value_before_shift:

`value_before_shift[31:0] = (rg_exp_mant << 16)[31:0]` = `{rg_exp_mant[15:0], 16'b0}`

Then `value_after_shift[31:0] = value_before_shift[31:0] >> shift_amount`

// 4:2 compressor, compressing 4 inputs into sum and carry
// `operands_i` is a flat vector: element k occupies bits [k*WIDTH_I +: WIDTH_I].
// (Flat rather than a 2-D packed array so yosys's Verilog front end can read it.)
module compressor_4to2 #(
    parameter int unsigned WIDTH_I = 8,                             // bit-width of inputs
    parameter int unsigned WIDTH_O = WIDTH_I + pdpu_pkg::clog2(4)   // bit-width of outputs
)(
    input logic [4*WIDTH_I-1:0] operands_i,
    output logic [WIDTH_O-1:0] sum_o,
    output logic [WIDTH_O-1:0] carry_o
);
    logic [WIDTH_I-1:0] sum;
    logic [WIDTH_I:0] cin;
    logic [WIDTH_I-1:0] cout;
    logic [WIDTH_I-1:0] carry;
    
    assign cin[0] = 1'b0;

    // Cascaded 5:3 counters according to input bit-width
    generate
        genvar i;
        for(i=0;i<WIDTH_I;i++) begin
            counter_5to3 u_counter_5to3(
                .x1(operands_i[0*WIDTH_I + i]),
                .x2(operands_i[1*WIDTH_I + i]),
                .x3(operands_i[2*WIDTH_I + i]),
                .x4(operands_i[3*WIDTH_I + i]),
                .cin(cin[i]),
                .sum(sum[i]),
                .carry(carry[i]),
                .cout(cout[i])
            );
            assign cin[i+1] = cout[i];
        end
    endgenerate

    logic [1:0] carry_temp;
    
    assign sum_o = sum;
    assign carry_temp = carry[WIDTH_I-1]+cin[WIDTH_I];
    assign carry_o = {carry_temp,carry[WIDTH_I-2:0],1'b0};
endmodule

// Recursive implementation of carry-save-adder (CSA) tree, to compress inputs with any size into sum and carry

    parameter int unsigned N = 8,           // size of inputs
    parameter int unsigned WIDTH_I = 8,     // bit-width of inputs

[ASSISTANT]
Good, reverted. Now let me try a different optimization strategy. Instead of optimizing the comp_tree itself, let me try to balance the pipeline better. The comp_tree (stage 1→2) is 763ps. What if I can split the comp_tree across two stages? I could compute partial maxes in stage 1 and the final max in stage 1.5 (registered) and then use it in stage 2.

But this would change the pipeline to 6 stages. The spec says "5 pipeline stages (pipe1..pipe5)". So I need to keep exactly 5 stages.

Let me instead try to reduce the work on the critical path stages by moving things around. For example:
- Stage 1 currently does: booth multiplier + comp_tree (max exponent)
- The booth multiplier output (mul_sum, mul_carry) is not on the critical path (it's fast for 4-bit × 4-bit)
- The comp_tree is the bottleneck

What if I move the exponent addition (rg_exp_c = rg_exp_a + rg_exp_b) from stage 0 to stage 1? Currently stage 0 computes rg_exp_c before pipe1. If I register rg_exp_a and rg_exp_b instead, and compute rg_exp_c in stage 1, the comp_tree would have one more adder delay in stage 1, making it worse.

Alternatively, what if I precompute the exponent max in stage 0 (before pipe1)? The comp_tree would need rg_exp_c which requires rg_exp_a + rg_exp_b (done in stage 0). The issue is that the comp_tree is already in stage 0's logic. Currently stage 0 does: posit decoding → exponent addition → register. If I add the comp_tree to stage 0, it would be: posit decoding → exponent addition → comp_tree → register, which makes stage 0 much heavier. But stage 0's current delay might be low enough to accommodate this.

Let me check: what's the current stage 0 delay? The inputs are combinational (not registered), so stage 0's logic (posit_decoder → exponent addition → pipe1 registers) must be within the clock period. But in a pipelined design, the combinational logic before the first register (pipe1) is also constrained by the clock. So if stage 0 is light, moving comp_tree there could help.

Actually wait - the pipeline stages are:
- Stage 0 (before pipe1): posit decoding + exponent addition → pipe1
- Stage 1 (pipe1→pipe2): booth multiplier + comp_tree → pipe2
- Stage 2 (pipe2→pipe3): mul add + alignment + complement → pipe3
- Stage 3 (pipe3→pipe4): CSA tree + final add → pipe4
- Stage 4 (pipe4→pipe5): normalization + encoder setup → pipe5
- Stage 5 (pipe5→output): posit encoder

If I move comp_tree from stage 1 to stage 0, stage 0 becomes: posit decoding + exponent addition + comp_tree → pipe1. Stage 1 becomes just the booth multiplier → pipe2.

But I'd need to register rg_exp_max into pipe1, and also register rg_exp_items (for computing the shift in stage 2). Let me try this:

// Recursive implementation of carry-save-adder (CSA) tree, to compress inputs with any size into sum and carry
// `operands_i` is a flat vector: element k occupies bits [k*WIDTH_I +: WIDTH_I].
// (Flat rather than a 2-D packed array so yosys's Verilog front end can read it.)

[ASSISTANT]
## Summary

### Starting Point
- `starting_point.v`: Baseline cost **968,045** (delay 910ps, area 1064). Critical path: pipe2→pipe3 (mantissa alignment + two's complement).

### What Worked Best (Final cost: **775,210**, 19.9% reduction)

1. **Two's complement → ones' complement + popcount carry-in** (biggest single win: 968K→936K→856K). Replaced `mantissa_comp = sign ? ~mant + 1 : mant` with `mant_comp = sign ? ~mant : mant` (ones' complement, no adder), then injected the `+1` carry-ins as `popcount(signs)` added to the final sum. Eliminated 17 full-width carry-propagate adders from the critical path.

2. **Simplified comparator** (856K→817K): Replaced the case-based signed comparator with a direct `assign result_o = (a >= b) ? a : b`. Yosys maps this to far better logic.

3. **Folded popcount into CSA tree** (817K→795K): Instead of `csa_sum + csa_carry + popcount` (3-operand add), fed popcount as an 18th operand into the CSA tree, leaving only a 2-operand final add.

4. **Replaced recursive CSA tree with for-loop sum** (795K→775K): The recursive `csa_tree` module with 4:2 compressors had inefficient carry chains. A simple `always_comb` for-loop sum let Yosys optimize the adder tree directly.

5. **Barrel shifter rewrite** (775K): Cleaned up the barrel shifter to use proper indexed stages instead of the ambiguous `temp_results` (no index on result_o).

### What Didn't Work
- **Truncating barrel shifter width** in posit_encoder (46→32 bits): broke correctness — upper bits of rg_exp_mant matter when shift is small.
- **Flat always_comb for comp_tree**: Yosys synthesized a 2500ps sequential chain instead of a tree.
- **Moving comp_tree to stage 0**: Made stage 0 path 1321ps (posit decode + exp add + max all combinational).
- **target_delay sweep**: 750ps and 1500ps targets both increased delay; tool doesn't respond well.
- **Simplifying comp_tree structure** (inlining comparators): slightly worse than original recursive.

### Lessons Learned
- **Ones' complement + deferred carry** is a powerful technique for signed accumulation — removes adders from critical paths.
- **Letting Yosys build adder trees** (for-loop) can beat hand-structured CSA trees with complex compressor modules.
- **Pipeline rebalancing** requires care: moving logic to an already-heavy stage (stage 0 with posit decoding) makes things worse, not better.
- The remaining critical path is the 5-level comparator tree (pipe1→pipe2, 763ps); a faster max-finder or restructuring the exponent processing could help further.
